"zero-based half-open characters in extracted text", "extracted_characters": 15180, "selected_characters": 10625, "extraction": "UTF-8 text", "selection_reason": "Ranked 4000-character windows by current entry title/tags/questions and algorithm/mechanism keywords; ties retain source order."}, {"path": "knowledge/system-semantics/host-runtime.md", "sha256": "005342b6131acdd072d5cf35456a5b01e5630f764b614e7090639fde01a5b36a", "reading_scope": "full", "category": "related_chapter", "content": "# K-SYS-015：服务主机运行时：执行域、队列与缓冲所有权\n\n推理服务的 CPU 工作包括接收与解析、tokenization、调度、张量准备、提交、结果处理和响应编码。它们有不同的阻塞方式、并行度和生命期，必须与模型算子共享同一份主机资源预算。\n\n## 问题、不变量与设计轴\n\n问题：设备每步只需要主机提交几次工作，但主机还要同时处理连接、分词、调度决策、采样后处理和响应编码；这些工作有的阻塞在 I/O，有的占满一个核，有的必须与设备完成事件同步。要回答的是\"哪些工作放在哪个执行域，域之间用什么通信、容量多大，缓冲属于谁、何时可回收\"。\n\n不变量：每个阶段的队列有界，溢出有明确的拒绝或等待语义；缓冲在同一时刻只有一个写者，跨域传递时所有权转移；取消能到达等待中的任一阶段；主机总 CPU 与内存不超过实际额度（容器 quota、cpuset）；设备提交不被与设备无关的主机工作阻塞。\n\n成本项：跨域通信（锁、队列、IPC 序列化）、上下文切换与缓存污染、每阶段服务时间与并行度的乘积、缓冲驻留（对象大小 × 同时存活数）、以及为了隔离付出的额外进程与复制。\n\n设计轴：执行域划分（单事件循环 / 线程池分阶段 / 多进程 / 混合）；通信方式（共享内存队列 / 管道 / 消息）；队列容量的依据（对象大小与存活范围 / 固定深度 / 按时间）；背压方向（上游拒绝 / 下游丢弃 / 两端协商）；设备提交线程（专用 / 与调度合一）。单进程事件循环加专用提交线程是小 batch 交互服务的常见点；多进程分词与编码在 CPU 占比高时出现。轴上未列点同样合法。\n\n## 何时重新设计执行域\n\n从单请求实现扩展到多请求服务，或观察到设备空闲但请求仍在等待、长输入延迟其他连接、增加 worker 后尾延迟反而上升、取消无法到达等待中的阶段时，主机运行时提供一组可调查的机制。每阶段的工作量与服务时间、真实 CPU/内存额度、原生库线程、设备提交方式和队列占用，可以随原型、profiling 与实验逐步获得，用来区分阻塞、资源争用和通信成本。\n\n执行域划分改变共享状态的更新与协调方式、跨域消息的字节、借用缓冲的生存期以及关闭时的未完工作。顺序执行、锁或原子操作协调的共享状态、消息传递可以形成不同实现，其取舍来自实际争用、搬运和错误传播成本。外部可观察行为见 [服务协议](service-protocol.md)；具体文件和符号位置由当前实现确定。\n\n## 执行域与有界通信\n\n事件循环适合协调大量等待中的连接和完成通知；一个长时间占用该执行域的解析或分词任务会延迟其他连接处理。线程池可以隔离阻塞工作，但是否获得 CPU 并行还取决于运行时和所调用的原生库。进程池增加隔离和独立执行能力，同时引入 IPC、序列化、地址空间和模型副本问题。线程数或进程数本身不是吞吐量。\n\n一个可分析的划分是：网络接入处理请求；输入处理形成带身份的 token/模态张量；调度组织就绪工作；模型执行消费资源；响应处理交付结果。这些是可合并或拆分的责任，既可以在同一调用链完成，也可以跨线程或进程。跨域后，需要解释请求与数据如何对应、谁可更新共享状态以及何时可重用缓冲；具体协调机制随设计变化。\n\n推进方式也可单独选择。由调用方驱动一次工作并消费结果，便于控制观察点；长期执行域接收请求并持续推进，则可能重叠准备、设备执行与结果处理，同时增加排队、通知和在途状态。两种方式都可以支持批处理，也可以混合使用。把同步调用改成返回等待对象，本身没有增加独立工作；收益取决于哪些依赖可以重叠，以及为此增加多少缓冲和协调成本。\n\n任务要求在过载下保持资源有界时，需要约束能够同时进入各阶段的总在途工作。队列容量、上游准入、端到端额度或其他资源管理机制都可以承担这一作用，不必为每条队列设置独立上限。计量单位可以是项数、字节、token 或估计工作量；仅限制请求数会把 100 个短提示与 100 个长视频当作相同负载。若上游生产速率持续高于下游消费速率且没有其他限制，持续积压会增加内存和尾延迟。达到所选预算边界时，可以等待、拒绝或采用任务允许的降载方式，同时保持 deadline 与取消的可达路径。\n\n## 统一计算 CPU 与内存预算\n\n设阶段 j 每请求平均消耗 cⱼ 个 CPU 秒，分配 pⱼ 个有效核，单看该阶段的稳定性必要条件为 λcⱼ<pⱼ。它忽略了方差和排队，因此不足以保证尾延迟。多个阶段共用 CPU 时还要满足总预算，另计模型内部线程、垃圾回收、通信和操作系统工作。\n\n设定 tokenization 每请求占用 8 ms CPU，输入域有两个有效核，其理想上限为 250 请求/秒；事件循环还有每请求 1 ms 的不可重叠 CPU 工作，理想上限为 1000 请求/秒。假设模型可以处理 500 请求/秒，当前输入域已经限制整体。把模型线程翻倍不会消除这个瓶颈；若大量长提示使分词成本翻倍，输入容量还会下降。\n\n主机内存要计入连接缓冲、未分词输入、token 数组、共享 IPC 段、pinned staging 和待发送输出。共享内存省去一次复制，但生产者不能在消费者结束前重用它；进程异常退出后，也需要由明确的资源拥有者回收。复制路径虽然多搬字节，却可能简化生命期并改善隔离，应比较数据大小、频率与错误处理成本。\n\n## 从取消到可回收\n\n| 请求位置 | 取消后的动作 | 仍需保护的对象 |\n|---|---|---|\n| 尚在输入队列 | 移除或跳过该代请求 | 仍被其他阶段读取的输入缓冲 |\n| 正在分词或预处理 | 标记结果不再提交，必要时协作停止 | 原生调用在用的输入与输出 |\n| 已提交模型执行 | 禁止后续调度与客户端提交 | 在途 DMA、kernel、通信和 KV |\n| 输出等待发送 | 终止发送，释放已无读者的结果 | 网络层尚未完成的缓冲访问 |\n\n取消标志不能倒转已发起的 DMA，也不能保证一个阻塞调用立即返回。迟到结果携带原请求及代次，不能写进已复用的槽位；最终清理要汇合各执行域的完成事件。对断开连接的响应行为以 [本地 ASGI 规范](../sources/S-ASGI.md) 的相应版本为准，不能把发送函数返回等同于客户端已经消费数据。\n\n## 背压如何连接到模型调度\n\n慢客户端会让输出积累。若每个请求的输出缓冲有上限，服务可以暂停该请求后续生成或按协议终止；暂停又会延长 KV 占用时间。因此网络背压也参与容量准入。批量刷出多个 token 降低封包开销，但增加可见延迟，并改变 ITL 事件位置；不能在没有说明口径时与逐 token 刷出比较。\n\n主机轮询减少唤醒延迟，却占用核和功耗；阻塞等待节省空闲资源，却引入唤醒与调度延迟。完成通知的批处理也存在同样取舍。对延迟敏感的小请求，调度和序列化可能占主导；对大张量，复制与内存带宽可能占主导。两类负载应通过测量决定执行域和缓冲设计。\n\n## 观测与反例\n\n沿同一请求记录各阶段入队、开始、结束、提交和取消事件，区分排队时间与 CPU 服务时间。检查 CPU 实际可用额度、进程/线程数、上下文切换、主存流量和分配次数，避免用“CPU 总利用率不高”排除单一执行域饱和。\n\n验证应包括长输入阻塞短输入、慢客户端、突发到达、队列饱和、分词失败、worker 退出和各阶段取消。比较纯复制与共享缓冲、单域与分域、请求并行与算子并行时固定任务语义和资源总预算。改善单阶段吞吐而使模型饥饿、内存峰值或请求尾延迟恶化，说明资源划分仍需调整。\n\n## 有效核数与线程组织的候选比较\n\n可见的 CPU 数量不等于可持续使用的 CPU 时间。Linux 固定 cgroup v2 文档中，cpuset.cpus.effective 表示实际获准使用的在线 CPU，cpu.max 表示一个周期内可消费的 CPU 时间上限；后者与前者约束不同。还需考虑祖先层级、其他竞争者及实际节流，才能估计当前执行域的可用预算。\n\n设定某服务允许在 8 个 CPU 上运行，CPU 时间额度为每 100 ms 消费 400 ms，且没有更严格的祖先限制。其长期 CPU 时间上限相当于 4 个核，并不保证任何一个线程随时都能获得运行时间。设每请求前处理需要 10 ms CPU，网络与解析需要 2 ms，调度和响应需要 3 ms；在 200 请求/s 下，这三项合计消耗 3 CPU 秒/s。若模型提交、通信及其他后台工作另需 1.2 CPU 秒/s，总需求为 4.2，已超过设定额度，增加 worker 数量不能消除这项约束。\n\n可以比较较少请求 worker 配合适量算子线程，以及更多请求 worker 配合更少算子线程。前者可能改善每次模型调用并减少跨域通信，后者可能提高独立请求的并行机会；原生库是否释放语言运行时限制、算子是否真正并行、cache 和 NUMA 局部性都会改变结果。两个并行 worker 各自允许 4 个算子线程，表示可能同时争用更多执行机会，不表示获得了额外 CPU 配额。\n\n验证需要同时保留各阶段 CPU 服务时间、等待时间、cpu.stat 的使用/节流记录、线程活跃情况和客户端尾延迟。固定 PyTorch CPU 指南提供 NUMA、OpenMP 线程与亲和性的调节依据，但其示例设置不能直接代替当前任务测量。若设备执行已经饱和且主机充足，增加主机并发的收益可能很小；若单执行域阻塞明显，适当拆分可能比扩张模型线程更有效。\n\n## 队列容量必须连接对象大小与同时存活范围\n\n| 队列或缓冲 | 合适的容量观察量 | 容量满时应传播到哪里 |\n|---|---|---|\n| 尚未完成输入解释 | 原始字节、媒体大小及待处理工作 | 入口读取、请求准入或输入期限 |\n| 已完成前处理 | token/张量字节、已保留请求数 | 前处理生产者与模型准入 |\n| 已提交的一次执行 | 在途批数、借用输入与暂定状态字节 | 调度器与状态分配器 |\n| 待交付输出 | 内容字节、媒体时长或事件数量 | 响应消费者、后续生成与新准入 |\n| 完成与取消通知 | 尚未处理的控制事件及其责任 | 相应状态拥有者，避免被数据队列永久阻塞 |\n\n有界数据队列也可能阻断终止路径。例如，执行线程必须把结果写入已满队列后才检查取消，而输出线程在等待取消确认时停止取走结果，两者会彼此等待。让等待入队可被取消、由仍可运行的控制路径处理取消，或在排空期间持续消费结果，形成不同解除依赖的方案。独立控制队列或预留通知容量有助于保持消息可达，但仍要保证处理通知的执行域没有被同一个阻塞操作占住。\n\n设定输入队列最多保留 512 个请求，每个原始输入上限 64 KiB，则该层原始字节上限为 32 MiB。若前处理结果还允许另有 512 个、每个最多 4096 个 4 字节 token ID，则该层为 8 MiB；128 个输出队列各保留 64 KiB，又增加 8 MiB。在三类对象能够同时达到上限、且互不共享存储的设定下，这三项合计为 48 MiB，尚未包含 Python/容器元数据、模态张量、IPC、pinned 缓冲与模型状态。\n\n如果处理阶段在生成新表示后立即交还原表示，可以缩短两份对象同时存活的时间；如果只是传递共享内存句柄，同一物理字节不应在物理容量中重复加算，但每个使用者仍有独立生命周期。进程之间复制模型、序列化中间张量或保留调试快照，会产生新的物理副本，应另列。平均请求很小不能证明有界内存，必须用准入允许的输入与同时存活关系计算。\n\n队列上限还会影响延迟。例如固定 worker 服务时间为 5 ms、前面已有 20 个需要串行服务的任务时，忽略其他延迟的等待已为 100 ms；若剩余 deadline 小于此值，继续进入同一队列不会使请求按时完成。真实任务有不同工作量，需按其可估计服务需求与不确定性判断，不能把此设定算例当成所有输入的等待预测。\n\n## 进程输出、超时与关闭也是运行时路径\n\n将预处理或执行放到子进程后，控制消息、结果、stdout/stderr 和进程状态是不同通道。固定 Python subprocess 文档说明，只等待进程结束而不消费有容量限制的输出管道，可能因管道写满形成死锁；一次通信超时也不一定终止子进程。长期 worker 还应避免把无界日志全部缓存在内存中。具体排空方法依赖所用运行时，本章不要求一种统一函数调用。\n\n关闭顺序由对象责任决定：停止新准入，告知拥有者停止未来工作，继续处理能够解除引用的完成事件，最后确认进程与设备相关操作已到达可回收边界。数据队列饱和时，关闭和取消通知仍必须可达。对于无法立即中断的原生调用或设备提交，可以撤销其结果提交资格，再等待完成；将等待超时标成清理成功，会使下一代 worker 继承未知责任。\n\n局部验证检查队列容量、消息归属和重复完成；集成验证在输入、执行、输出分别设置迟到与取消，并加入正在正常运行的邻居请求；系统验证覆盖突发、长短混合、慢消费、日志洪峰和 worker 异常。最终检查各执行域的待办数、实际 CPU 时间、队列字节及共享/私有资源，而不是只用主进程的“活跃请求为零”判定排空。设备上的完成与内存条件参见 [CUDA stream 与分配器](../frameworks/streams-allocator.md)。\n\n<!-- infraagents:provenance -->\n\n## 来源与推导\n\n- [S-ASGI：ASGI — HTTP and WebSocket Message Format](../sources/S-ASGI.md)：ASGI HTTP/WebSocket 2026-04-30；本次编辑日期不改变来源时间。\n\n## 延伸阅读\n\n- [CPU 推理：线程、缓存层级与 NUMA 分区](../hardware/cpu-serving.md)。\n- [服务协议：接收、流式背压与终止语义](../system-semantics/service-protocol.md)。\n- [取消与完成：业务终止、设备排空和资源回收](../system-semantics/cancellation-resource-completion.md)。\n- [请求身份：逻辑序列、批次视图与结果归属](../system-semantics/request-batch-identity.md)。\n- [可观测性：执行事件与采集事件的映射](../tools/trace-observability.md)。\n- [异步调度：依赖图、代次与双缓冲](../algorithms/async-scheduling.md)。\n- [系统设计推导：模型、缓存、执行与目标的组合](design-synthesis.md)。\n", "excerpt_ranges": [[0, 7559]], "range_unit": "zero-based half-open characters in extracted text", "extracted_characters": 7559, "selected_characters": 7559, "extraction": "UTF-8 text", "selection_reason": "full extracted text fits allocation"}, {"path": "knowledge/tools/statistical-experiments.md", "sha256": "908b3588a6fa20bb1f2aed9f5db51c4b462953d79f8944cdbcfea7141c74644f", "reading_scope": "full", "category": "related_chapter", "content": "# K-TOOLS-007：性能实验：独立重复、效应量与尾部证据\n\n性能结论要同时回答改了多少、波动来自哪里，以及结论适用于哪组条件。更多计时样本只有在增加了与问题相关的信息时才提高可信度；同一进程内反复测量不能自动替代重启、重新构建或不同时间段的重复。\n\n## 先固定比较的对象\n\n声明比较的是冷启动、稳态单请求、固定到达率服务，还是容量边界。版本、硬件与配额、模型、输入/输出分布、缓存条件、质量标准和测量事件必须一致。把预打包移出计时是可以研究的稳态设定，但需要另报一次性成本；它不能直接支持冷启动更快的结论。\n\n对于越小越好的延迟，既可以报告差值 A−B，也可以报告比值 A/B，但要说明用的是均值、分位数还是每轮比值的聚合。均值的比值、比值的均值与分位数的比值不是同一估计量。吞吐也要包含完成窗口、失败和未完成工作，而不是只选快速成功的请求。\n\n## 重复的层级\n\n一次实验包含环境/时间段、进程或模型实例、请求/迭代等层级。布局、编译、NUMA、热状态和后台竞争可以在不同层级引入变化。[效应量置信区间研究](../sources/S-STATS.md) §6–7 将这种层级变化与重复次数、比值区间连接起来；不能把其假设之外的任意轨迹直接代入公式。\n\n如果只有进程内反复采样，而进程间差异很大，继续增加内层重复会得到看似稳定但覆盖范围很窄的均值。先用少量分层试验辨别主要变异，再把预算投到有效层级。稳态预热规则需要事先确定或用独立预实验建立；若系统始终存在周期和漂移，应报告这种行为，而非挑一段最快区间称为稳态。\n\n## 配对、区组与区间\n\n对可重复的请求轨迹，可以在同一个时间区组内运行 A/B，并随机或均衡安排先后，控制环境漂移。配对的单位应是独立运行或区组，而不是把同一运行里强相关的每个 token 当作独立样本。若两方案会改变后续生成内容，应区分固定前缀计算对照与真实生成质量/性能对照。\n\n在独立配对差值 dⱼ 近似满足相应 t 区间条件时，均值差的区间为 d̄±t·s𝒹/√n。设定 16 个独立区组，平均降低 2 ms、差值样本标准差为 1 ms，使用自由度 15 的双侧 95% 临界值约 2.131，区间约为 [1.467,2.533] ms。这是给定统计量的算例；它既不证明某实现有该收益，也不是“未来单请求延迟落在其中的概率”。\n\n层级 bootstrap 应保留实际层级与配对关系。时间相关的数据需要考虑成块重采样或更高层独立重复，不能随机打散相关结构后制造很窄的区间。少量独立块、强漂移、极端长尾会限制区间可信度；无论用解析方法还是重采样，都需要说明独立性、平稳性与样本范围。\n\n## 尾延迟与失败不能被均值掩盖\n\np99 依赖尾部的少量样本。100 个请求的经验 p99 只有很有限的尾部信息，重复报告多个小数位不会增加精度。超时是被删失或失败的观测，不应悄悄删除；如果 SLO 定义要求在 deadline 内成功，超时和质量失败应按该定义计入。\n\n对固定阈值的超限率，假设 n 个独立、同分布请求且观察到零超限，单侧置信度 1−α 的上界为 1−α^(1/n)，来自零超限概率 (1−q)ⁿ。n=100、α=0.05 时上界约 2.95%；所以零失败 100 次并不能支持真实超限率低于 1% 的同等置信结论。这个二项式算例在突发相关、负载漂移或选择性停止时不能照搬。\n\n服务试验还应展示 offered load、队列增长、完成数与错误数。有限时间内的 goodput 不代表长期稳定；若队列持续增长，测量窗口结束时尚未完成的请求是关键证据。\n\n## 搜索后的验证与停止条件\n\n从很多候选中挑最好的结果，会同时挑中有利噪声。探索用的轨迹、形状与调参集应与最终保留验证分开；确定候选后冻结代码和配置，在独立运行上重测，并报告试过的候选范围。失败的正确性检查不能因为性能好而被排除。\n\n停止条件围绕实际决策设定：例如在质量通过且 SLO 不退化的前提下，收益是否超过值得维护的最小幅度。区间跨过决策边界时，证据不足以决定；应增加有效重复或说明未区分，而不是把单次最好值当成结论。持续查看结果直到恰好显著再停止，会改变统计含义，需要预先设计或使用相应的序贯方法。\n\n发布结论保留原始事件、运行身份、失败样本、聚合定义及不确定性。一次局部 kernel 对照仅支持该 shape 与条件；知识库是否提高 Agent 设计能力，还需要固定任务、模型和预算，对比旧 base、扩充 base 与无知识条件，并隔离各自运行学习。\n\n## 针对知识库作用安排受控构建实验\n\n下面是研究者可选择的对照设计，统计重复原则受上述固定来源支持，具体 Agent 条件不归称为来源论文的实验，也不构成 Agent 构建时必须执行的流程。研究知识条件差异时，可比较无知识、旧 seed、扩充 seed；研究运行学习时，可以另设 learned 条件。每组固定实际角色模型、任务语义、可用工具、基础代码起点、外部资料权限和预算口径，并在批次开始前冻结知识版本与评价器。各组拥有独立的案例与发布状态，防止先运行的组把经验传给后运行的组。\n\n主要结果包括固定预算内完整引擎的成功交付率、到第一个合格系统的成本、到目标性能的成本、以及合格系统的端到端表现。固定预算完成率中，未交付均计为未成功并保留原因。若另分析到达合格系统的时间，仍可能完成、且仅因预先统一的时间窗口结束而停止的运行，可以记为行政性右删失；崩溃或不可恢复失败应另列失败事件，不能任意按独立删失处理。其他资源预算导致的停止也需解释删失机制。不能只平均成功任务的完成时间而不说明选择范围。模型生成存在随机性，独立重复的单位是完整构建运行，而不是一个运行中的多个工作项。\n\n检索命中、正文读取和知识使用声明用于检查访问链路；预期设计改变是否进入实现，需要单独核对。若扩充组减少无效实验且在相同验收标准下实测总成本或到达合格系统的成本下降，即使未提高最终吞吐，也可以支持构建成本改善；仅减少实验次数并不充分，因为剩余实验可能更昂贵。若交付率提高却消耗更多资源，则应展示相应权衡，不能只报告最好的一条轨迹。\n\n扩充内容中的示范任务与算例不应直接成为唯一最终测试集。开发场景与独立保留场景分开，按模型结构、硬件能力、负载和状态交互说明哪些条件发生了变化。硬件不能全部交叉时报告实际测试子集，避免将一个设备的结果外推为跨平台收益。\n\n## 检查测量链是否会接受不可能的提升\n\n性能测量的负对照保持任务标准不变，主动制造应该使结果失格的轨迹：缺失响应却缩短总时间、提前结束测量仍有在途请求、只保留快请求、在 benchmark 中跳过真实模型路径。测量应拒绝这些轨迹或明确记录缺失/失败，而不是给出更好的成功分数。\n\n计量完成点与质量完成点应绑定到同一批请求和候选版本。profiling 的同步、额外复制或影子参考会扰动执行；用于诊断的计数与最终正常路径的性能分开报告。原始数据若不足以重建规定的统计量，结论是未测得，不以另一个聚合量替代。\n\n<!-- infraagents:provenance -->\n\n## 来源与推导\n\n- [S-STATS：Quantifying Performance Changes with Effect Size Confidence Intervals](../sources/S-STATS.md)：§6–7 连接多层随机影响、实验重复、效应量和置信区间，并明确方法假设。\n  [完整原文](../../references/S-STATS/reader.html) · [全文文本](../../references/S-STATS/fulltext.txt)\n\n- [S-MLPERF-SCENARIOS：MLCommons — Inference Mobile Scenarios](../sources/S-MLPERF-SCENARIOS.md)：不同推理场景使用不同请求驱动方式，例如前一响应完成后再发与独立到达。相同平均吞吐并不表示相同负载压力。\n  [算法与机制说明](../sources/S-MLPERF-SCENARIOS.md)\n\n- [S-GENAI-PERF：NVIDIA GenAI-Perf](../sources/S-GENAI-PERF.md)：GenAI-Perf 的指标围绕请求、响应、首个内容、流式间隔、输出长度和测量窗口定义。客户端 chunk 与模型 token 并非必然一一对应。\n  [完整原文](../../references/S-GENAI-PERF/reader.html) · [全文文本](../../references/S-GENAI-PERF/fulltext.txt)\n\n- [S-TORCH-REPRO：PyTorch — Reproducibility](../sources/S-TORCH-REPRO.md)：随机种子与确定性配置可以控制特定执行路径中的随机性，但不保证跨版本、平台和全部算法路径逐位一致。\n Available in Nsight Systems Embedded Platforms Edition only.\n--vulkan-gpu-workload\ntrue, false, individual,\n\nbatch, none\nindividual\nIf individual or true, trace each Vulkan workload’s GPU activity individually. If batch, trace\n\nVulkan workloads’ GPU activity in vkQueueSubmit call batches. If none or false, do not trace Vulkan\n\nworkloads’ GPU activity. This option is not supported on QNX.\n--wait\nprimary,all\nall\nIf primary, the CLI will wait on the application process termination. If all, the CLI will\n\nadditionally wait on re-parented processes created by the application.\n--wddm-additional-events\ntrue, false\ntrue\nIf true, collect additional range of ETW events, including context status, allocations, sync wait\n\nand signal events, etc. Note that this switch is applicable only when\n--trace=wddm\nis specified.\n\nThis option is only supported on Windows targets.\n--wddm-backtraces\ntrue, false\nfalse\nIf true, collect backtraces of WDDM events. This option is only supported on Windows targets.\n--xhv-trace\n< filepath pct.json >\nnone\nCollect hypervisor trace. Available in Nsight Systems Embedded Platforms Edition only.\n--xhv-trace-events\nall, none, core, sched,\n\nirq, trap\nall\nAvailable in Nsight Systems Embedded Platforms Edition only.\nCLI Analyze Command Switch Options\n\nThe\nnsys\nanalyze\ncommand generates and outputs to the terminal a report using expert system r\n\n[EXCERPT extracted-character range 52000:72000]\n Only available on Grace.\n--cpu-socket-events\n0x2a, 0x2c, …, none\nnone\nCollect per-socket Uncore PMU counters. Multiple values can be selected, separated by commas only (no spaces).\n\nUse the\n--cpu-socket-events=help\nswitch to see the full list of values. Available in Nsight Systems Embedded Platforms Edition only.\n--cpuctxsw\nprocess-tree, system-wide,\n\nnone\nprocess-tree\nTrace OS thread scheduling activity. Select ‘none’ to disable tracing CPU context switches. Depending on\n\nthe platform, some values may require admin or root privileges. Note: if the\n--sample\nswitch is set\n\nto a value other than ‘none’, the\n--cpuctxsw\nsetting is hardcoded to the same value as the\n--sample\nswitch. If\n--sample=none\nand a target application is launched, the default is ‘process-tree’, otherwise\n\nthe default is ‘none’. Requires\n--sampling-trigger=perf\nswitch in Nsight Systems Embedded Platforms Edition\n--cuda-flush-interval\nmilliseconds\nSee Description\nSet the interval, in milliseconds, when buffered CUDA data is automatically saved to storage. CUDA data\n\nbuffer saves may cause profiler overhead. Buffer save behavior can be controlled with this switch. If the\n\nCUDA flush interval is set to 0 on systems running CUDA 11.0 or newer, buffers are saved when they fill.\n\nIf a flush interval is set to a non-zero value on such systems, buffers are saved only when the flush\n\ninterval expires. If a flush interval is set and the profiler runs out of available buffers before the\n\nflush interval expires, additional buffers will be allocated as needed. In this case, setting a flush\n\ninterval can reduce buffer save overhead but increase memory use by the profiler. If the flush interval\n\nis set to 0 on systems running older versions of CUDA, buffers are saved at the end of the collection. If\n\nthe profiler runs out of available buffers, additional buffers are allocated as needed. If a flush interval\n\nis set to a non-zero value on such systems, buffers are saved when the flush interval expires. A\n\ncuCtxSynchronize call may be inserted into the workflow before the buffers are saved which will cause\n\napplication overhead. In this case, setting a flush interval can reduce memory use by the profiler but may\n\nincrease save overhead. For collections over 30 seconds an interval of 10 seconds is recommended. Default is\n\n10000 for Nsight Systems Embedded Platforms Edition and 0 otherwise.\n--cuda-memory-usage\ntrue, false\nfalse\nTrack the GPU memory usage by CUDA kernels. Applicable only when CUDA tracing is enabled. Note: This feature\n\nmay cause significant runtime overhead.\n--cuda-um-cpu-page-faults\ntrue, false\nfalse\nThis switch tracks the page faults that occur when CPU code tries to access a memory page that resides on the\n\ndevice. Note that this feature may cause significant runtime overhead. Not available on Nsight Systems Embedded Platforms Edition.\n--cuda-um-gpu-page-faults\ntrue, false\nfalse\nThis switch tracks the page faults that occur when GPU code tries to access a memory page that resides on the\n\nhost. Note that this feature may cause significant runtime overhead. Not available on Nsight Systems Embedded Platforms Edition.\n--cudabacktrace\nall, none, kernel, memory,\n\nsync, other\nnone\nWhen tracing CUDA APIs, enable the collection of a backtrace when a CUDA API is invoked. Significant runtime\n\noverhead may occur. Values may be combined using ‘,’. Each value except ‘none’ may be appended with a\n\nthreshold after ‘:’. Threshold is duration, in nanoseconds, that CUDA APIs must execute before backtraces\n\nare collected, e.g. ‘kernel:500’. Default value for each threshold is 1000ns (1us). Note: CPU sampling must\n\nbe enabled.\n--cuda-graph-trace\ngraph, node\ngraph\nIf ‘graph’ is selected, CUDA graphs will be traced as a whole and node activities will not be collected. This\n\nwill reduce overhead to a minimum, but requires CUDA driver version 515.43 or higher. If ‘node’ is selected,\n\nnode activities will be collected, but CUDA graphs will not be traced as a whole. This may cause significant\n\nruntime overhead. The percentage can be greater\n\nthan 100.\n--isr\ntrue, false\nfalse\nTrace Interrupt Service Routines (ISRs) and Deferred Procedure Calls (DPCs). Requires administrative\n\nprivileges. Available only on Windows devices.\n--nic-metrics\ntrue, false\nfalse\nCollect metrics from supported NIC/HCA devices. System scope. Not available on Nsight Systems Embedded Platforms Edition.\n--os-events\n‘help’ or the end users\n\nselected events in the\n\nformat ‘x,y’\nSelect the OS events to sample. Use the\n--os-events=help\nswitch to see the full list of events.\n\nMultiple values can be selected, separated by commas only (no spaces). Use the\n--event-sample\nswitch to enable. Not available on Nsight Systems Embedded Platforms Edition.\n-o\n--output\n< filename >\nreport#\nSet report file name. Any %q{ENV_VAR} pattern in the filename will be substituted with the value\n\nof the environment variable. Any %h pattern in the filename will be substituted with the hostname\n\nof the system. Any %p pattern in the filename will be substituted with the PID of the target\n\nprocess or the PID of the root process if there is a process tree. Any %% pattern in the filename\n\nwill be substituted with %. Default is report#{.nsys-rep,.sqlite,.h5,.txt,.arrows,_arwdir,_pqtdir,.json}\n\nin the working directory.\n--process-scope\nmain, process-tree,\n\nsystem-wide\nmain\nSelect which process(es) to trace. Available in Nsight Systems Embedded Platforms Edition only. Nsight Systems Workstation Edition will always\n\ntrace system-wide in this version of the tool.\n--retain-etw-files\ntrue, false\nfalse\nRetain ETW files generated by the trace, merge and move the files to the output directory.\n-s\n--sample\nprocess-tree, system-wide,\n\nnone\nprocess-tree\nSelect how to collect CPU IP/backtrace samples. If ‘none’ is selected, CPU sampling is disabled.\n\nDepending on the platform, some values may require admin or root privileges. If a target application\n\nis launched, the default is ‘process-tree’, otherwise, the default is ‘none’. Note: ‘system-wide’\n\nis not available on all platforms. Note: If set to ‘none’, CPU context switch data will still be\n\ncollected unless the\n--cpuctxsw\nswitch is set to ‘none’.\n--samples-per-backtrace\ninteger <= 32\n1\nThe number of CPU IP samples collected for every CPU IP/backtrace sample collected. For example,\n\nif set to 4, on the fourth CPU IP sample collected, a backtrace will also be collected. Lower\n\nvalues increase the amount of data collected. Higher values can reduce collection overhead and\n\nreduce the number of CPU IP samples dropped. If DWARF backtraces are collected, the default is 4,\n\notherwise the default is 1. This option is not available on Nsight Systems Embedded Platforms Edition or on non-Linux targets.\n--sampling-frequency\n100 < integers < 8000\n1000\nSpecify the sampling/backtracing frequency. The minimum supported frequency is 100 Hz. The maximum\n\nsupported frequency is 8000 Hz. This option is supported only on QNX, Linux for Tegra, and Windows\n\ntargets.\n--sampling-period\n(Nsight Systems Embedded Platforms Edition)\ninteger\ndetermined dynamically\nThe number of CPU Cycle events counted before a CPU instruction pointer (IP) sample is collected.\n\nIf configured, backtraces may also be collected. The smaller the sampling period, the higher the\n\nsampling rate. Note that smaller sampling periods will increase overhead and significantly increase\n\nthe size of the result file(s). Requires\n--sampling-trigger=perf\nswitch.\n--sampling-period\n(not Nsight Systems Embedded Platforms Edition)\ninteger\ndetermined dynamically\nThe number of events counted before a CPU instruction pointer (IP) sample is collected. The event\n\nused to trigger the collection of a sample is determined dynamically. For example, on Intel based\n\nplatforms, it will probably be “Reference Cycles” and on AMD platforms, “CPU Cycles”. If configured,\n\nbacktraces may also be collected. The smaller the sampling period, the higher the sampling rate.\n\nNote that smaller sampling periods will increase overhead and significantly increase the size of\n\nthe result file(s). This option is available only on Linux targets.\n--sampling-trigger\ntimer, sched, perf, cuda\ntimer,sched\nSpecify backtrace collection trigger. Multiple APIs can be selected, separated by commas only\n\n(no spaces). Available on Nsight Systems Embedded Platforms Edition targets only.\n--session-new\n[a-Z][0-9,a-Z,spaces]\nprofile-<id>-<application>\nName the session created by the command. Name must start with an alphabetical character followed by\n\nprintable or space characters. Any\n%q{ENV_VAR}\npattern will be substituted with the value of the\n\nenvironment variable. Any\n%h\npattern will be substituted with the hostname of the system. Any\n%%\npattern will be substituted with\n%\n.\n-w\n--show-output\ntrue, false\ntrue\nIf true, send target process’s stdout and stderr streams to both the console and stdout/stderr files\n\nwhich are added to the report file. If false, only send target process stdout and stderr streams to\n\nthe stdout/stderr files which are added to the report file.\n--soc-metrics\ntrue,false\nfalse\nCollect SoC Metrics. Available in Nsight Systems Embedded Platforms Edition only.\n--soc-metrics-frequency\ninteger\n10000\nSpecify SoC Metrics sampling frequency. Minimum supported frequency is ‘100’ (Hz). Maximum supported\n\nfrequency is ‘1000000’ (Hz). Available in Nsight Systems Embedded Platforms Edition only.\n--soc-metrics-set\nalias, file:<file name>\nsee description\nSpecify metric set for SoC Metrics. The argument must be one of the aliases reported by\n--soc-metrics-set=help\nswitch, or a path to a metric config file prefixed by\nfile:\n.\n\nAvailable in Nsight Systems Embedded Platforms Edition only.\n--stats\ntrue, false\nfalse\nGenerate summary statistics after the collection. WARNING: When set to true, an SQLite database will\n\nbe created after the collection. If the collection captures a large amount of data, creating the\n\ndatabase file may take several minutes to complete.\n-x\n--stop-on-exit\ntrue, false\ntrue\nIf true, stop collecting automatically when the launched process has exited or when the duration\n\nexpires - whichever occurs first. If false, duration must be set and the collection stops only when\n\nthe duration expires. Nsight Systems does not officially support runs longer than 5 minutes.\n--vsync\ntrue, false\nfalse\nCollect vsync events. If collection of vsync events is enabled, display/display_scanline ftrace\n\nevents will also be captured. Available in Nsight Systems Embedded Platforms Edition only.\n--xhv-trace\n< filepath pct.json >\nnone\nCollect hypervisor trace. Available in Nsight Systems Embedded Platforms Edition only.\n--xhv-trace-events\nall, none, core, sched,\n\nirq, trap\nall\nAvailable in Nsight Systems Embedded Platforms Edition only.\nCLI Stats Command Switch Options\n\nThe\nnsys\nstats\ncommand generates a series of summary or trace reports.\n\nThese reports can be output to the console, or to individual files, or piped to\n\nexternal processes. Reports can be rendered in a variety of different output\n\nformats, from human readable columns of text, to formats more appropriate for\n\ndata exchange, such as CSV.\nReports are generated from an SQLite export of a .nsys-rep file. If a .nsys-rep\n\nf\n\n[EXCERPT extracted-character range 96000:104000]\nnone\nPrint the status of the indicated session. The option argument must represent a valid session name or ID as reported by\nnsys\nsessions\nlist\n. Any\n%q{ENV_VAR}\npattern will be substituted with the value of the environment variable. Any\n%h\npattern will be substituted with the hostname of the system. Any\n%%\npattern will be substituted with\n%\n.\nCLI Stop Command Switch Options\n\nAfter choosing the\nstop\ncommand switch, the following options are available. Usage:\nnsys\n[\nglobal\n-\noptions\n]\nstop\n[\noptions\n]\nShort\nLong\nPossible Parameters\nDefault\nSwitch Description\n--help\n<tag>\nnone\nPrint the help message. The option can take one optional argument that will be used as a tag. If a tag is provided, only options relevant to the tag will be printed.\n--session\nsession identifier\nnone\nStop the indicated session. The option argument must represent a valid session name or ID as reported by\nnsys\nsessions\nlist\n. Any\n%q{ENV_VAR}\npattern will be substituted with the value of the environment variable. Any\n%h\npattern will be substituted with the hostname of the system. Any\n%%\npattern will be substituted with\n%\n.\nExample Single Command Lines\n\nVersion Information\nnsys -v\nEffect: Prints tool version information to the screen.\nRun with elevated privilege\nsudo nsys profile <app>\nEffect: Nsight Systems CLI (and target application) will run with elevated privilege. This is necessary for some features, such as FTrace or system-wide CPU sampling. If you don’t want the target application to be elevated, use\n--run-as\noption.\nDefault analysis run\nnsys profile <application>\n\n    [application-arguments]\nEffect: Launch the application using the given arguments. Start collecting immediately and end collection when the application stops. Trace CUDA, OpenGL, NVTX, and OS runtime libraries APIs. Collect CPU sampling information and thread scheduling information. With Nsight Systems Embedded Platforms Edition this will only analysis the single process. With Nsight Systems Workstation Edition this will trace the process tree. Generate the report#.nsys-rep file in the default location, incrementing the report number if needed to avoid overwriting any existing output files.\nLimited trace only run\nnsys profile --trace=cuda,nvtx -d 20\n\n    --sample=none --cpuctxsw=none -o my_test <application>\n\n    [application-arguments]\nEffect: Launch the application using the given arguments. Start collecting immediately and end collection after 20 seconds or when the application ends. Trace CUDA and NVTX APIs. Do not collect CPU sampling information or thread scheduling information. Profile any child processes. Generate the output file as my_test.nsys-rep in the current working directory.\nDelayed start run\nnsys profile -e TEST_ONLY=0 -y 20\n\n    <application> [application-arguments]\nEffect: Set environment variable TEST_ONLY=0. Launch the application using the given arguments. Start collecting after 20 seconds and end collection at application exit. Trace CUDA, OpenGL, NVTX, and OS runtime libraries APIs. Collect CPU sampling and thread schedule information. Profile any child processes. Generate the report#.nsys-rep file in the default location, incrementing if needed to avoid overwriting any existing output files.\nCollect ftrace events\nnsys profile --ftrace=drm/drm_vblank_event\n\n    -d 20\nEffect: Collect ftrace\ndrm_vblank_event\nevents for 20 seconds. Generate the report#.nsys-rep file in the current working directory. Note that ftrace event collection requires running as root. To get a list of ftrace events available from the kernel, run the following:\nsudo cat /sys/kernel/debug/tracing/available_events\nRun GPU metric sampling on one TU10x\nnsys profile --gpu-metrics-devices=0\n\n    --gpu-metrics-set=tu10x-gfxt <application>\nEffect: Launch application. Collect default options and GPU metrics for the first GPU (a TU10x), using the tu10x-gfxt metric set at the default frequency (10 kHz). Profile any child processes. Generate the report#.nsys-rep file in the default location, incrementing if needed to avoid overwriting any existing output files.\nRun GPU metric sampling on all GPUs at a set frequency\nnsys profile --gpu-metrics-devices=all\n\n    --gpu-metrics-frequency=20000 <application>\nEffect: Launch application. Collect default options and GPU metrics for all available GPUs using the first suitable metric set for each and sampling at 20 kHz. Profile any child processes. Generate the report#.nsys-rep file in the default location, incrementing if needed to avoid overwriting any existing output files.\nCollect CPU IP/backtrace and CPU context switch\nnsys profile --sample=system-wide --duration=5\nEffect: Collects both CPU IP/backtrace samples using the default backtrace mechanism and traces CPU context switch activity for the whole system for 5 seconds. Note that it requires root permission to run. No hardware or OS events are sampled. Post processing of this collection will take longer due to the large number of symbols to be resolved caused by system-wide sampling.\nGet list of available CPU core events\nnsys profile --cpu-core-events=help\nEffect: Lists the CPU events that can be sampled and the maximum number of CPU events that can be sampled concurrently.\nCollect system-wide CPU events and trace application\nnsys profile --event-sample=system-wide\n\n    --cpu-core-events='1,2' --event-sampling-frequency=5 <app> [app args]\nEffect:Collects CPU IP/backtrace samples using the default backtrace mechanism, traces CPU context switch activity, and samples each CPU’s “CPU Cycles” and “Instructions Retired” event every 200 ms for the whole system. Note that it requires root permission to run. Note that CUDA, NVTX, OpenGL, and OSRT within the app launched by Nsight Systems are traced by default while using this command. Post processing of this collection will take longer due to the large number of symbols to be resolved caused by system-wide sampling.\nCollect custom ETW trace using configuration file\nnsys profile --etw-provider=file.JSON\nEffect: Configure custom ETW collectors using the contents of file.JSON. Collect data for 20 seconds. Generate the report#.nsys-rep file in the current working directory.\nA template JSON configuration file is located at in the Nsight Systems installation directory as \\target-windows-x64\\etw_providers_template.json. This path will show up automatically if you call\nnsys profile --help\nThe\nlevel\nattribute can only be set to one of the following:\nTRACE_LEVEL_CRITICAL\nTRACE_LEVEL_ERROR\nTRACE_LEVEL_WARNING\nTRACE_LEVEL_INFORMATION\nTRACE_LEVEL_VERBOSE\nThe\nflags\nattribute can only be set to one or more of the following:\nEVENT_TRACE_FLAG_ALPC\nEVENT_TRACE_FLAG_CSWITCH\nEVENT_TRACE_FLAG_DBGPRINT\nEVENT_TRACE_FLAG_DISK_FILE_IO\nEVENT_TRACE_FLAG_DISK_IO\nEVENT_TRACE_FLAG_DISK_IO_INIT\nEVENT_TRACE_FLAG_DISPATCHER\nEVENT_TRACE_FLAG_DPC\nEVENT_TRACE_FLAG_DRIVER\nEVENT_TRACE_FLAG_FILE_IO\nEVENT_TRACE_FLAG_FILE_IO_INIT\nEVENT_TRACE_FLAG_IMAGE_LOAD\nEVENT_TRACE_FLAG_INTERRUPT\nEVENT_TRACE_FLAG_JOB\nEVENT_TRACE_FLAG_MEMORY_HARD_FAULTS\nEVENT_TRACE_FLAG_MEMORY_PAGE_FAULTS\nEVENT_TRACE_FLAG_NETWORK_TCPIP\nEVENT_TRACE_FLAG_NO_SYSCONFIG\nEVENT_TRACE_FLAG_PROCESS\nEVENT_TRACE_FLAG_PROCESS_COUNTERS\nEVENT_TRACE_FLAG_PROFILE\nEVENT_TRACE_FLAG_REGISTRY\nEVENT_TRACE_FLAG_SPLIT_IO\nEVENT_TRACE_FLAG_SYSTEMCALL\nEVENT_TRACE_FLAG_THREAD\nEVENT_TRACE_FLAG_VAMAP\nEVENT_TRACE_FLAG_VIRTUAL_ALLOC\nTypical case: profile a Python script that uses CUDA\nnsys profile --trace=cuda,cudnn,cublas,osrt,nvtx\n\n    --delay=60 python my_dnn_script.py\nEffect: Launch a Python script and start profiling it 60 seconds after the launch, tracing CUDA, cuDNN, cuBLAS, OS runtime APIs, and NVTX as well as collecting thread schedule information.\nTypical case: profile an app that uses Vulkan\nnsys profile --trace=vulkan,osrt,nvtx\n\n    --delay=60 ./myapp\nEffect: Launch an app and start profiling it 60 seconds after the launch, tracing Vulkan, OS runtime APIs, and NVTX as well as collecting CPU sampling and thread schedule information.\nExample Interactive CLI Command Sequences\n\nCol\n\n[EXCERPT extracted-character range 108000:116000]\nt range with message “profiler” is opened in the default domain.\nBy default only messages, provided by NVTX registered strings are considered to avoid additional overhead. To enable non-registered strings check please launch your application with\nNSYS_NVTX_PROFILER_REGISTER_ONLY=0\nenvironment:\nnsys launch -w true -p profiler@service -e NSYS_NVTX_PROFILER_REGISTER_ONLY=0 ./app\nNote\nThe separator ‘@’ can be escaped with backslash ‘\\’. If multiple separators without escape character are specified, only the last one is applied, all others are discarded.\nRun application, start/stop collection multiple times\nThe interactive CLI supports multiple sequential collections per launch.\nnsys launch <application> [application-arguments]\n\nnsys start\n\nnsys stop\n\nnsys start\n\nnsys stop\n\nnsys shutdown --kill sigkill\nEffect: Create interactive CLI and launch an application set up for default analysis. Send application output to the terminal. No data is collected until the start command is executed. Collect data from start until stop requested, generate report#.qstrm in the current working directory. Collect data from second start until the second stop request, generate report#.nsys-rep (incremented by one) in the current working directory. Shutdown the interactive CLI and send sigkill to the target application’s process group.\nNote\nCalling\nnsys\ncancel\nafter\nnsys\nstart\nwill cancel the collection without generating a report.\nExample Stats Command Sequences\n\nDisplay default statistics\nnsys\nstats\nreport1.nsys-rep\nEffect: Export an SQLite file named report1.sqlite from report1.nsys-rep (assuming it does not already exist). Print the default reports in column format to the console.\nNote: The following two command sequences should present very similar information:\nnsys\nprofile\n--stats=true\n<application>\nor\nnsys\nprofile\n<application>\nnsys\nstats\nreport1.nsys-rep\nDisplay specific data from a report\nnsys\nstats\n--report\ncuda_gpu_trace\nreport1.nsys-rep\nEffect: Export an SQLite file named report1.sqlite from report1.nsys-rep (assuming it does not already exist). Print the report generated by the\ncuda_gpu_trace\nscript to the console in column format.\nGenerate multiple reports, in multiple formats, output multiple places\nnsys\nstats\n--report\ncuda_gpu_trace\n--report\ncuda_gpu_kern_sum\n--report\ncuda_api_sum\n--format\ncsv,column\n--output\n.,-\nreport1.nsys-rep\nEffect: Export an SQLite file named report1.sqlite from report1.nsys-rep (assuming it does not already exist). Generate three reports. The first, the\ncuda_gpu_trace\nreport, will be output to the file report1_cuda_gpu_trace.csv in CSV format. The other two reports,\ncuda_gpu_kern_sum\nand\ncuda_api_sum\n, will be output to the console as columns of data. Although three reports were given, only two formats and outputs are given. To reconcile this, both the list of formats and outputs is expanded to match the list of reports by repeating the last element.\nSubmit report data to a command\nnsys\nstats\n--report\ncuda_api_sum\n--format\ntable\n\\\n--output\n@“grep\n-E\n(-|Name|cudaFree”\ntest.sqlite\nEffect: Open test.sqlite and run the\ncuda_api_sum\nscript on that file. Generate table data and feed that into the command\ngrep\n-E\n(-|Name|cudaFree)\n. The grep command will filter out everything but the header, formatting, and the\ncudaFree\ndata, and display the results to the console.\nNote: When the output name starts with @, it is defined as a command. The command is run, and the output of the report is piped to the command’s stdin (standard-input). The command’s stdout and stderr remain attached to the console, so any output will be displayed directly to the console.\nBe aware there are some limitations in how the command string is parsed. No shell expansions (including *, ?, [], and ~) are supported. The command cannot be piped to another command, nor redirected to a file using shell syntax. The command and command arguments are split on whitespace, and no quotes (within the command syntax) are supported. For commands that require complex command line syntax, it is suggested that the command be put into a shell script file, and the script designated as the output command\nExample Output from\n--stats\nOption\n\nThe\nnsys\nstats\ncommand can be used post analysis to generate specific or personalized reports. For a default fixed set of summary statistics to be automatically generated, you can use the\n--stats\noption with the\nnsys\nprofile\nor\nnsys\nstart\ncommand to generate a fixed set of useful summary statistics.\nIf your run traces CUDA, these include CUDA API, Kernel, and Memory Operation statistics:\nIf your run traces OS runtime events or NVTX push-pop ranges:\nIf your run traces graphics debug markers these include DX11 debug markers, DX12 debug markers, Vulkan debug markers or KHR debug markers:\nRecipes for these statistics as well as documentation on how to create your own metrics will be available in a future version of the tool.\nSystem Wide API Trace on Windows\n\nOn Windows, Nsight Systems can trace certain APIs (currently supported: DX11, DX12\n\nand Vulkan) in already-running applications, by way of system-wide API trace from\n\nthe CLI.\nTo initiate system-wide API tracing, run the Nsight Systems CLI with the\n--trace\noption including one or more of the supported APIs, the\n--system-wide``option\nset\nto\n``true\n, and without specifying a target\n\napplication. System-wide API tracing may be combined with trace types that are\n\nalways system-wide such as\n--trace=wddm\n.\nTo trace a DX11 or DX12 target application, it must gain the system focus, the\n\nuser must either click on the application window or using Alt+Tab to select it.\nFor example, to trace multiple DX12 applications with PIX markers and GPU\n\nworkload trace as well as WDDM events for the next 20 seconds, run the command:\nnsys profile --trace=dx12-annotations,wddm --dx12-gpu-workload=individual\n\n--duration=20\nthen click each of the target applications’ windows to give them focus.\nTo trace a Vulkan target application, it must be launched after the\nnsys\nprofile\ncommand has been executed.\nOpening Command Line Results Files for Visualization\n\nOpen CLI results in GUI\nThe CLI will generate an .nsys-rep file after analysis is complete. This file can\n\nbe opened in any GUI that is the same version or a more recent version.\nThe opening of really large, multi-gigabyte, .nsys-rep files may take up all of\n\nthe memory on the host computer and lock up the system. This will be fixed in a\n\nlater version.\nImporting Windows ETL files\nFor Windows targets, ETL files captured with Xperf or the\nlog.cmd\ncommand\n\nsupplied with GPUView in the Windows Performance Toolkit can be imported to\n\ncreate reports as if they were captured with Nsight Systems’s “WDDM trace” and\n\n“Custom ETW trace” features. Simply choose the .etl file from the Import dialog\n\nto convert it to a .nsys-rep file.\nHandling Application Launchers (mpirun, deepspeed, etc)\n\nNsight Systems has built-in API trace support for various communication APIs,\n\nsuch as MPI, OpenSHMEM, UCX, NCCL and NVSHMEM.\n\nTo execute respective programs on multiple different machines (compute nodes),\n\nusually launchers are used, e.g.\nmpirun\n/\nmpiexec\n(MPI),\nshmemrun\n/\noshrun\n(OpenSHMEM),\nsrun\n(SLURM) or\ndeepspeed\n.\nIn\nsingle-node profiling\nsessions, the Nsight Systems CLI can be prefixed before\n\nthe program (binary) or the launcher. In the latter case, the execution of the\n\nlauncher will also be profiled and all processes will be recorded into the same\n\nreport file, e.g for mpirun\nnsys\nprofile\n[nsys\nargs]\nmpirun\n[mpirun\nargs]\n...\nIn\nmulti-node profiling\nsessions, the Nsight Systems CLI has to be prefixed\n\nbefore the application, but not before the launcher command, e.g. for mpirun\nmpirun\n[mpirun\nargs]\nnsys\nprofile\n[nsys\nargs]\n...\nYou can use\n%q{OMPI_COMM_WORLD_RANK}\n(Open MPI),\n%q{PMI_RANK}\n(MPICH),\n%q{SLURM_PROCID}\n(Slurm) or\n%p\nin the\n-o|--output\noption to include\n\nthe rank or process ID into the report file name.\nWarning:\nAn error will occur, if several processes want to write to the same report file\n\n\n[EXCERPT extracted-character range 120000:124000]\n\nfirewall\n-\ncmd\n--\nlist\n-\nports\n--\npermanent\nsudo\nfirewall\n-\ncmd\n--\nreload\nTo open a port use the following command, skip\n--permanent\noption to open only for this session:\nsudo\nfirewall\n-\ncmd\n--\npermanent\n--\nadd\n-\nport\n45555\n/\ntcp\nsudo\nfirewall\n-\ncmd\n--\nreload\nLikewise, if you are running on a cloud system, you must open port 22 and port 45555 for ingress.\nKernel Version Number\n- To check for the version number of the kernel support of Nsight Systems on a target device, run the following command on the remote device:\ncat\n/\nproc\n/\nquadd\n/\nversion\nMinimal supported version is 1.82.\nAdditionally, presence of Netcat command (\nnc\n) is required on the target device. For example, on Ubuntu this package can be installed using the following command:\nsudo\napt\n-\nget\ninstall\nnetcat\n-\nopenbsd\nSystem-Wide Profiling Options\n\nTarget Sampling Options\n\nTarget sampling behavior is somewhat different for Nsight Systems Workstation Edition and Nsight Systems Embedded Platforms Edition.\nHotkey Trace Start/Stop\n\nNsight Systems Workstation Edition can use hotkeys to control profiling. Press the hotkey to start and/or stop a trace session from within the target application’s graphic window. This is useful when tracing games and graphic applications that use fullscreen display. In these scenarios switching to Nsight Systems’ UI would unnecessarily introduce the window manager’s footprint into the trace. To enable the use of Hotkey check the Hotkey checkbox in the project settings page:\nThe default hotkey is F12.\nLaunching Processes\n\nNsight Systems can launch new processes for profiling on target devices. Profiler ensures that all environment variables are set correctly to successfully collect trace information\nThe\nEdit arguments…\nlink will open an editor window, where every command line argument is edited on a separate line. This is convenient when arguments contain spaces or quotes.\nProfiling Windows Targets from the GUI\n\nProfiling on Windows devices is similar to the profiling on Linux devices. Please refer to the\nProfiling Linux Targets from the GUI\nsection for the detailed documentation and connection information. The major differences on the platforms are listed below:\nRemoting to a Windows Based Machine\n\nTo perform remote profiling to a target Windows based machines,\ninstall and configure an OpenSSH Server on the target machine.\nHotkey Trace Start/Stop\n\nNsight Systems Workstation Edition can use hotkeys to control profiling. Press the hotkey to start and/or stop a trace session from within the target application’s graphic window. This is useful when tracing games and graphic applications that use fullscreen display. In these scenarios switching to Nsight Systems’ UI would unnecessarily introduce the window manager’s footprint into the trace. To enable the use of Hotkey check the Hotkey checkbox in the project settings page:\nThe default hotkey is F12.\nChanging the Default Hotkey Binding\n- A different hotkey binding can be configured by setting the HotKeyIntValue configuration field in the config.ini file.\nSet the decimal numeric identifier of the hotkey you would like to use for triggering start/stop from the target app graphics window. The default value is 123 which corresponds to 0x7B, or the F12 key.\nVirtual key identifiers are detailed in\nMSDN’s Virtual-Key Codes\n.\nNote that you must convert the hexadecimal values detailed in this page to their decimal counterpart before using them in the file. For example, to use the F1 key as a start/stop trace hotkey, use the following settings in the config.ini file:\nHotKeyIntValue\n=\n112\nTarget Sampling Options on Windows\n\nNsight Systems can sample one process tree. Sampling here means interrupting each processor periodically. The sampling rate is defined in the project settings and is either 100Hz, 1KHz (default value), 2Khz, 4KHz, or 8KHz.\nOn Windows, Nsight Systems can collect thread activity of one process tree. Collecting thread activity means that each thread context switch event is logged an\n\n[EXCERPT extracted-character range 136000:140000]\nrofiler-users-guide\n.\nThe nvprof transition guide for Nsight Compute can be found at\nhttps://docs.nvidia.com/nsight-compute/NsightComputeCli/index.html#nvprof-guide\n.\nAny nvprof switch not listed below is not supported by the\nnsys\nnvprof\ncommand. No additional nsys functionality is available through this command. New features will not be added to this command in the future.\nCLI nvprof Command Switch Options\n\nAfter choosing the\nnvprof\ncommand switch, the following options are available. When you are ready to move to using Nsight Systems CLI directly, see\nCommand Line Options\ndocumentation for the nsys switch(es) given below. Note that the nsys implementation and output may vary from nvprof.\nUsage\n.\nnsys\nnvprof\n[\noptions\n]\nSwitch\nParameters (Default in Bold)\nnsys switch\nSwitch Description\n--annotate-mpi\noff\n, openmpi, mpich\n--trace=mpi\nAND\n--mpi-impl\nAutomatically annotate MPI calls with NVTX markers. Specify the MPI implementation installed on your machine. Only OpenMPI and MPICH implementations are supported.\n--cpu-thread-tracing\non,\noff\n--trace=osrt\nCollect information about CPU thread API activity.\n--profile-api-trace\nnone, runtime, driver,\nall\n--trace=cuda\nTurn on/off CUDA runtime and driver API tracing. For Nsight Systems there is no separate CUDA runtime and CUDA driver trace, so selecting\nruntime\nor\ndriver\nis equivalent to selecting\nall\n.\n--profile-from-start\non\n, off\nif off use\n--capture-range=cudaProfilerApi\nEnable/disable profiling from the start of the application. If disabled, the application can use {cu,cuda}Profiler{Start,Stop} to turn on/off profiling.\n-t\n--timeout\n<nanoseconds> default=\n0\n--duration=seconds\nIf greater than 0, stop the collection and kill the launched application after timeout seconds. nvprof started counting when the CUDA driver is initialized. nsys starts counting immediately.\n--cpu-profiling\non,\noff\n--sampling=cpu\nTurn on/off CPU profiling\n--openacc-profiling\non\n, off\n--trace=openacc\nto turn on\nEnable/disable recording information from the OpenACC profiling interface. Note: OpenACC profiling interface depends on the presence of the OpenACC runtime. For supported runtimes, see\nCUDA Trace\nsection of documentation\n-o\n--export-profile\n<filename>\n--output={filename}\nand/or\n--export=sqlite\nExport named file to be imported or opened in the Nsight Systems GUI. %q{ENV_VAR} in string will be replaced with the set value of the environment variable. If not set this is an error. %h in the string is replaced with the system hostname. %% in the string is replaced with %. %p in the string is not supported currently. Any other character following % is illegal. The default is report1, with the number incrementing to avoid overwriting files, in users working directory.\n-f\n--force-overwrite\n--force-overwrite=true\nForce overwriting all output files with same name.\n-h\n--help\n--help\nPrint Nsight Systems CLI help\n-V\n--version\n--version\nPrint Nsight Systems CLI version information\nNext Steps\n\nNVIDIA Visual Profiler (NVVP) and NVIDIA nvprof are deprecated. New GPUs and features will not be supported by those tools. We encourage you to make the move to Nsight Systems now. For additional information, suggestions, and rationale, see the blog series in\nOther Resources\n.\nDirect3D Trace\n\nNsight Systems has the ability to trace both the Direct3D 11 API and the Direct3D 12 API on Windows targets.\nD3D11 API trace\n\nNsight Systems can capture information about Direct3D 11 API calls made by the profiled process. This includes capturing the execution time of D3D11 API functions, performance markers, and frame durations.\nSLI Trace\nTrace SLI queries and peer-to-peer transfers of D3D11 applications. Requires SLI hardware and an active SLI profile definition in the NVIDIA console.\nD3D12 API Trace\n\nDirect3D 12 is a low-overhead 3D graphics and compute API for Microsoft Windows. Information about Direct3D 12 can be found at the\nDirect3D 12 Programming Guide\n.\nNsight Systems can capture information about Direct3D 12 usage by the prof\n\n[EXCERPT extracted-character range 144000:148000]\n\nWDDM Queues\n\nThe Windows Display Driver Model (WDDM) architecture uses queues to send work packets from the CPU to the GPU. Each D3D device in each process is associated with one or more contexts. Graphics, compute, and copy commands that the profiled application uses are associated with a context, batched in a command buffer, and pushed into the relevant queue associated with that context.\nNsight Systems can capture the state of these queues during the trace session.\nEnabling the “Collect additional range of ETW events” option will also capture extended DxgKrnl events from the\nMicrosoft-Windows-DxgKrnl\nprovider, such as context status, allocations, sync wait, signal events, etc.\nA command buffer in a WDDM queues may have one the following types:\nRender\nDeferred\nSystem\nMMIOFlip\nWait\nSignal\nDevice\nSoftware\nIt may also be marked as a Present buffer, indicating that the application has finished rendering and requests to display the source surface.\nSee the Microsoft documentation for the WDDM architecture and the DXGKETW_QUEUE_PACKET_TYPE enumeration.\nTo retain the .etl trace files captured, so that they can be viewed in other tools (e.g. GPUView), change the “Save ETW log files in project folder” option under “Profile Behavior” in Nsight Systems’s global Options dialog. The .etl files will appear in the same folder as the .nsys-rep file, accessible by right-clicking the report in the Project Explorer and choosing “Show in Folder…”. Data collected from each ETW provider will appear in its own .etl file, and an additional .etl file named “Report XX-Merged-*.etl”, containing the events from all captured sources, will be created as well.\nWDDM HW Scheduler\n\nWhen GPU Hardware Scheduling is enabled in Windows 10 or newer version, the Windows Display Driver Model (WDDM) uses the DxgKrnl ETW provider to expose report of NVIDIA GPUs’ hardware scheduling context switches.\nNsight Systems can capture these context switch events, and display under the GPUs in the timeline rows titled WDDM HW Scheduler - [HW Queue type]. The ranges under each queue will show the process name and PID assoicated with the GPU work during the time period.\nThe events will be captured if GPU Hardware Scheduling is enabled in the Windows System Display settings, and “Collect WDDM Trace” is enabled in the Nsight Systems Project Settings.\nVulkan API Trace\n\nVulkan Overview\n\nVulkan is a low-overhead, cross-platform 3D graphics and compute API, targeting a wide variety of devices from PCs to mobile phones and embedded platforms. The Vulkan API is defined by the Khronos Group. Information about Vulkan and the Khronos Group can be found at the\nKhronos Vulkan Site\n.\nNsight Systems can capture information about Vulkan usage by the profiled process. This includes capturing the execution time of Vulkan API functions, corresponding GPU workloads, debug util labels, and frame durations. Vulkan profiling is supported on both Windows and x86 Linux operating systems.\nThe Command Buffer Creation row displays time periods when command buffers were being created. This enables developers to improve their application’s multi-threaded command buffer creation. Command buffer creation time period is measured between the call to\nvkBeginCommandBuffer\nand the call to\nvkEndCommandBuffer\n.\nA Queue row is displayed for each Vulkan queue created by the profiled application. The API sub-row displays time periods where\nvkQueueSubmit\nwas called. The GPU Workload sub-row displays time periods where workloads were executed by the GPU.\nIn addition, you can see\nVulkan debug util labels\non both the CPU and the GPU.\nClicking on a GPU workload highlights the corresponding\nvkQueueSubmit\ncall, and vice versa.\nThe Vulkan Memory Operations row contains an aggregation of all the Vulkan host-side memory operations, such as host-blocking writes and reads or non-persistent map-unmap ranges.\nThe row is separated into sub-rows by heap index and memory type - the tooltip for each row and the ranges inside show the\n\n[EXCERPT extracted-character range 156000:164000]\nare\n\nshown as green markers while SYSMEM resources are shown in gray. Hovering over a\n\nmarker or selecting it in the\nEvents view\nwill display\n\nall the allocation parameters as well as the call stack that led to the\n\nallocation event.\nResource Migrations\nThis row displays a breakdown of resources’ movement between VRAM and SYSMEM,\n\nfocusing on resource evictions. The main row shows a timeline of total evicted\n\nresource memory and count as a red-colored line graph.\nEach child row displays a timeline of the status of each resource, as reflected\n\nby WDDM events related to it. If the object has been named using PIX or\nID3D11Object::SetName\n/\nID3D12Object::SetName\n, the name will be shown\n\nin the row title. Whether named or not, the row title will also show the\n\nresource dimensions, format, priority, and the memory size migrated. If the\n\nresource was migrated in parts using subresources, the row can be expanded to\n\nshow the status for each subresource at any given time.\nExpanding the row for a resource will show the individual WDDM events relevant\n\nto it and the call stacks that led to each event.\nBy default, the resources are sorted by Relevance (most / largest migrations).\n\nRight-clicking the main Resource Migrations row header allows choosing between\n\nthe following sorting options:\nRelevance\nName\nFormat\nPriority\nEarliest allocation timestamp (order of appearance on the host)\nEarliest migration timestamp (order of appearance on the device)\nThe top 5 resources are shown initially. If more than 5 resources exist, a row\n\nshowing the number of hidden resources and buttons allowing to show more or\n\nfewer of them will appear below them. Right-click this row and select “show all”\n\nor “show all collapsed” to display all the resources at once.\nMemory Transfer\nThis row shows an overview of all memory transfer operations. Device-to-host\n\ntransfers are shown in orange, host-to-device transfers are shown in green,\n\ndiscarded device memory is shown in light green, and unknown events are shown\n\nin dark gray. The height of each event marker corresponds to the amount of\n\nmemory that the event affected. Hovering over the marker will show the exact amount.\nExpanding the row will show a breakdown of the events by each specific type.\nVertical Synchronization\n\nThe VSYNC rows display when the monitor’s vertical synchronizations occur.\nOpenMP Trace\n\nNsight Systems for Linux is capable of capturing information about OpenMP events. This functionality is built on the OpenMP Tools Interface (OMPT), full support is available only for runtime libraries supporting tools interface defined in OpenMP 5.0 or greater.\nAs an example, LLVM OpenMP runtime library partially implements tools interface. If you use PGI compiler <= 20.4 to build your OpenMP applications, add -mp=libomp switch to use LLVM OpenMP runtime and enable OMPT based tracing. If you use Clang, make sure the LLVM OpenMP runtime library you link to was compiled with tools interface enabled.\nOnly a subset of the OMPT callbacks are processed:\nompt_callback_parallel_begin\nompt_callback_parallel_end\nompt_callback_sync_region\nompt_callback_task_create\nompt_callback_task_schedule\nompt_callback_implicit_task\nompt_callback_master\nompt_callback_reduction\nompt_callback_task_create\nompt_callback_cancel\nompt_callback_mutex_acquire\n,\nompt_callback_mutex_acquired\nompt_callback_mutex_acquired\n,\nompt_callback_mutex_released\nompt_callback_mutex_released\nompt_callback_work\nompt_callback_dispatch\nompt_callback_flush\nNote\nThe raw OMPT events are used to generate ranges indicating the runtime of OpenMP operations and constructs.\nExample screenshot:\nOS Runtime Libraries Trace\n\nOn Linux, OS runtime libraries can be traced to gather information about low-level userspace APIs. This traces the system call wrappers and thread synchronization interfaces exposed by the C runtime and POSIX Threads (pthread) libraries. This does not perform a complete runtime library API trace, but instead focuses on the functions that can take a long time to execute, or could potentially cause your thread be unscheduled from the CPU while waiting for an event to complete. OS runtime trace is not available for Windows targets.\nOS runtime tracing complements and enhances sampling information by:\nVisualizing when the process is communicating with the hardware, controlling resources, performing multi-threading synchronization or interacting with the kernel scheduler.\nAdding additional thread states by correlating how OS runtime libraries traces affect the thread scheduling:\nWaiting\n— the thread is not scheduled on a CPU, it is inside of an OS runtime libraries trace and is believed to be waiting on the firmware to complete a request.\nIn OS runtime library function\n— the thread is scheduled on a CPU and inside of an OS runtime libraries trace. If the trace represents a system call, the process is likely running in kernel mode.\nCollecting backtraces for long OS runtime libraries call. This provides a way to gather blocked-state backtraces, allowing you to gain more context about why the thread was blocked so long, yet avoiding unnecessary overhead for short events.\nTo enable OS runtime libraries tracing from Nsight Systems:\nCLI\n— Use the\n-t\n,\n--trace\noption with the\nosrt\nparameter. See\nCommand Line Options\nfor more information.\nGUI\n— Select the\nCollect OS runtime libraries trace\ncheckbox.\nYou can also use\nSkip if shorter than\n. This will skip calls shorter than the given threshold. Enabling this option will improve performances as well as reduce noise on the timeline. We strongly encourage you to skip OS runtime libraries call shorter than 1 μs.\nLocking a Resource\n\nThe functions listed below receive a special treatment. If the tool detects that the resource is already acquired by another thread and will induce a blocking call, we always trace it. Otherwise, it will never be traced.\npthread_mutex_lock\npthread_rwlock_rdlock\npthread_rwlock_wrlock\npthread_spin_lock\nsem_wait\nNote that even if a call is determined as potentially blocking, there is a chance that it may not actually block after a few cycles have elapsed. The call will still be traced in this scenario.\nLimitations\n\nNsight Systems only traces syscall wrappers exposed by the C runtime. It is not able to trace syscall invoked through assembly code.\nAdditional thread states, as well as backtrace collection on long calls, are only enabled if sampling is turned on.\nIt is not possible to configure the depth and duration threshold when collecting backtraces. Currently, only OS runtime libraries calls longer than 80 μs will generate a backtrace with a maximum of 24 frames. This limitation will be removed in a future version of the product.\nIt is required to compile your application and libraries with the\n-funwind-tables\ncompiler flag in order for Nsight Systems to unwind the backtraces correctly.\nOS Runtime Libraries Trace Filters\n\nThe OS runtime libraries tracing is limited to a select list of functions. It also depends on the version of the C runtime linked to the application.\nOS Runtime Default Function List\n\nLibc system call wrappers\naccept\naccept4\nacct\nalarm\narch_prctl\nbind\nbpf\nbrk\nchroot\nclock_nanosleep\nconnect\ncopy_file_range\ncreat\ncreat64\ndup\ndup2\ndup3\nepoll_ctl\nepoll_pwait\nepoll_wait\nfallocate\nfallocate64\nfcntl\nfdatasync\nflock\nfork\nfsync\nftruncate\nfutex\nioctl\nioperm\niopl\nkill\nkillpg\nlisten\nmembarrier\nmlock\nmlock2\nmlockall\nmmap\nmmap64\nmount\nmove_pages\nmprotect\nmq_notify\nmq_open\nmq_receive\nmq_send\nmq_timedreceive\nmq_timedsend\nmremap\nmsgctl\nmsgget\nmsgrcv\nmsgsnd\nmsync\nmunmap\nnanosleep\nnfsservctl\nopen\nopen64\nopenat\nopenat64\npause\npipe\npipe2\npivot_root\npoll\nppoll\nprctl\npread\npread64\npreadv\npreadv2\npreadv64\nprocess_vm_readv\nprocess_vm_writev\npselect6\nptrace\npwrite\npwrite64\npwritev\npwritev2\npwritev64\nread\nreadv\nreboot\nrecv\nrecvfrom\nrecvmmsg\nrecvmsg\nrt_sigaction\nrt_sigqueueinfo\nrt_sigsuspend\nrt_sigtimedwait\nsched_yield\nseccomp\nselect\nsemctl\nsemget\nsemop\nsemtimedop\nsend\nsendfile\nsendfile64\nsendmmsg\nsendmsg\nsendto\nshmat\ns\n\n[EXCERPT extracted-character range 168000:180000]\n the “Insert NVTX Marker hotkey” option it is possible\n\nto add NVTX markers to a running non-console applications by pressing the\n\nF11 key. These will appear in the report under the NVTX Domain named “HotKey\n\nmarkers”.\nTypically calls to NVTX functions can be left in the source code even if the\n\napplication is not being built for profiling purposes, since the overhead is\n\nvery low when the profiler is not attached.\nNVTX is not intended to annotate very small pieces of code that are being called\n\nvery frequently. A good rule of thumb to use: if code being annotated usually\n\ntakes less than 1 microsecond to execute, adding an NVTX range around this code\n\nshould be done carefully.\nNote\nRange annotations should be matched carefully. If many ranges are opened but\n\nnot closed, Nsight Systems has no meaningful way to visualize it. A rule of\n\nthumb is to not have more than a couple dozen ranges open at any point in\n\ntime. Nsight Systems does not support reports with many unclosed ranges.\nNVTX Payloads and Counters (Preview)\nNVTX Extended Payloads and NVTX Counters increase the flexibility of NVTX\n\nannotations by allowing users to pass arbitrary structured data\n\nto NVTX events. This then will allow users to define the layout of this data in\n\nthe Nsight Systems UI without additional data conversion.\nFor more information, see\nNVTX documentation\n.\nNVTX Domains and Categories\nNVTX domains enable scoping of annotations. Unless specified differently, all\n\nevents and annotations are in the default domain. Additionally, categories can\n\nbe used to group events.\nNsight Systems gives the user the ability to include or exclude NVTX events from\n\na particular domain. This can be especially useful if you are profiling across\n\nmultiple libraries and are only interested in nvtx events from some of them.\nThis functionality is also available from the CLI. See the CLI documentation\n\nfor\n--nvtx-domain-include\nand\n--nvtx-domain-exclude\nfor more details.\nCategories that are set in by the user will be recognized and displayed in the\n\nGUI.\nCUDA Trace\n\nBasic CUDA trace\n\nNsight Systems is capable of capturing information about CUDA execution in the\n\nprofiled process.\nThe following information can be collected and presented on the timeline in the\n\nreport:\nCUDA API trace — trace of CUDA Runtime and CUDA Driver calls made by the application.\nCUDA Runtime calls typically start with\ncuda\nprefix (e.g.\ncudaLaunch\n).\nCUDA Driver calls typically start with\ncu\nprefix (e.g.\ncuDeviceGetCount\n).\nCUDA workload trace — trace of activity happening on the GPU, which includes\n\nmemory operations (e.g., Host-to-Device memory copies) and kernel executions.\n\nWithin the threads that use the CUDA API, additional child rows will appear\n\nin the timeline tree.\nOn Nsight Systems Workstation Edition, cuDNN and cuBLAS API tracing and OpenACC tracing.\nNear the bottom of the timeline row tree, the GPU node will appear and contain\n\na CUDA node. Within the CUDA node, each CUDA context used within the process\n\nwill be shown along with its corresponding CUDA streams. Steams will contain\n\nmemory operations and kernel launches on the GPU. Kernel launches are\n\nrepresented by blue, while memory transfers are displayed in red.\nThe easiest way to capture CUDA information is to launch the process from\n\nNsight Systems, and it will setup the environment for you. To do so, simply set\n\nup a normal launch and select the\nCollect CUDA trace\ncheckbox.\nFor Nsight Systems Workstation Edition this looks like:\nFor Nsight Systems Embedded Platforms Edition this looks like:\nAdditional configuration parameters are available:\nCollect backtraces for API calls longer than X seconds\n- turns on\n\ncollection of CUDA API backtraces and sets the minimum time a CUDA API event\n\nmust take before its backtraces are collected. Setting this value too low can\n\ncause high application overhead and seriously increase the size of your\n\nresults file.\nFlush data periodically\n— specifies the period after which an attempt to\n\nflush CUDA trace data will be made. Normally, in order to collect full CUDA\n\ntrace, the application needs to finalize the device used for CUDA work (call\ncudaDeviceReset()\n, and then let the application gracefully exit (as\n\nopposed to crashing).\nThis option allows flushing CUDA trace data even before the device is\n\nfinalized. However, it might introduce additional overhead to a random CUDA\n\nDriver or CUDA Runtime API call.\nSkip some API calls\n— avoids tracing insignificant CUDA Runtime API calls\n\n(namely,\ncudaConfigureCall()\n,\ncudaSetupArgument()\n,\ncudaHostGetDevicePointers()\n). Not tracing these functions allows\n\nNsight Systems to significantly reduce the profiling overhead, without losing\n\nany interesting data.\nCollect GPU Memory Usage\n- collects information used to generate a graph\n\nof CUDA allocated memory across time. Note that this will increase overhead.\n\nSee\nCUDA GPU Memory Allocation Graph\n.\nCollect Unified Memory CPU page faults\n- collects information on page\n\nfaults that occur when CPU code tries to access a memory page that resides on\n\nthe device. See\nUnified Memory CPU Page Faults\n.\nCollect Unified Memory GPU page faults\n- collects information on page\n\nfaults that occur when GPU code tries to access a memory page that resides on\n\nthe CPU. See\nUnified Memory GPU Page Faults\n.\nCollect CUDA Graph trace\n- by default, CUDA tracing will collect and\n\nexpose information on a whole graph basis. The user can opt to collect on a\n\nnode per node basis. See\nCUDA Graph Trace\n.\nFor Nsight Systems Workstation Edition,\nCollect cuDNN trace\n,\nCollect cuBLAS trace\n,\nCollect OpenACC trace\n- selects which (if any) extra libraries that\n\ndepend on CUDA to trace.\nOpenACC versions 2.0, 2.5, and 2.6 are supported when using PGI runtime\n\nversion 15.7 or greater and not compiling statically. In order to differentiate\n\nconstructs, a PGI runtime of 16.1 or later is required. Note that\n\nNsight Systems Workstation Edition does not support the GCC implementation of OpenACC at this\n\ntime.\nNote\nIf your application crashes before all collected CUDA trace data has been\n\ncopied out, some or all data might be lost and not present in the report.\nNote\nNsight Systems will not have information about CUDA events that were still in\n\ndevice buffers when analysis terminated. It is a good idea, if using\n\ncudaProfilerAPI to control analysis to call cudaDeviceReset before ending\n\nanalysis.\nLaunching NVIDIA Nsight Compute from a CUDA Kernel\n\nAfter you have used CUDA trace in Nsight Systems to locate a potential problem\n\nkernel, you may want to run NVIDIA Nsight Compute on that specific kernel. This is\n\nnot the same as the GPU memory graph generated during stutter analysis on the\n\nWindows target. See\nWindows GPU Memory Utilization\nBelow, in the report on the left, memory is allocated and freed during the\n\ncollection. In the report on the right, memory is allocated, but not freed\n\nduring the collection.\nHere is another example, where allocations are happening on multiple GPUs\nUnified Memory Transfer Trace\n\nFor Nsight Systems Workstation Edition, Unified Memory (also called Managed Memory) transfer trace is enabled automatically in Nsight Systems when CUDA trace is selected. It incurs no overhead in programs that do not perform any Unified Memory transfers. Data is displayed in the Managed Memory area of the timeline:\nHtoD transfer\nindicates the CUDA kernel accessed managed memory that was residing on the host, so the kernel execution paused and transferred the data to the device. Heavy traffic here will incur performance penalties in CUDA kernels, so consider using manual cudaMemcpy operations from pinned host memory instead.\nPtoP transfer\nindicates the CUDA kernel accessed managed memory that was residing on a different device, so the kernel execution paused and transferred the data to this device. Heavy traffic here will incur performance penalties, so consider using manual cudaMemcpyPeer operations to transfer from other devices’ memory instead. The row showing these events is for the destination device - the source device is shown in the tooltip for each transfer event.\nDtoH transfer\nindicates the CPU accessed managed memory that was residing on a CUDA device, so the CPU execution paused and transferred the data to system memory. Heavy traffic here will incur performance penalties in CPU code, so consider using manual cudaMemcpy operations from pinned host memory instead.\nSome Unified Memory transfers are highlighted with red to indicate potential performance issues:\nTransfers with the following migration causes are highlighted:\nCoherence\nUnified Memory migration occurred to guarantee data coherence. SMs (streaming multiprocessors) stop until the migration completes.\nEviction\nUnified Memory migrated to the CPU because it was evicted to make room for another block of memory on the GPU. This happens due to memory overcommitment which is available on Linux with Compute Capability ≥ 6.\nUnified Memory CPU Page Faults\n\nThe Unified Memory CPU page faults feature in Nsight Systems tracks the page\n\nfaults that occur when CPU code tries to access a memory page that resides on\n\nthe device.\nNote\nCollecting Unified Memory CPU page faults can cause overhead of up to 70% in\n\ntesting. Please use this functionality only when needed.\nUnified Memory GPU Page Faults\n\nThe Unified Memory GPU page faults feature in Nsight Systems tracks the page\n\nfaults that occur when GPU code tries to access a memory page that resides on\n\nthe host.\nNote\nCollecting Unified Memory GPU page faults can cause overhead of up to 70% in\n\ntesting. Please use this functionality only when needed.\nCUDA Graph Trace\n\nNsight Systems is capable of capturing information about CUDA graphs in your\n\napplication at either the graph or node granularity. This can be set in the CLI\n\nusing the\n--cuda-graph-trace\noption, or in the GUI by setting the appropriate\n\ndrop down.\nWhen CUDA graph trace is set to\ngraph\n, the users sees each graph as one item\n\non the timeline:\nWhen CUDA graph trace is set to\nnode\n, the users sees each graph as a set of\n\nnodes on the timeline:\nTracing CUDA graphs at the graph level rather than the tracing the underlying\n\nnodes results in significantly less overhead. This option is only available\n\nith CUDA driver 515.43 or higher.\nCUDA Python Backtrace\n\nNsight Systems for Arm server (SBSA) platforms and x86 Linux targets, is capable of capturing Python backtrace information when CUDA backtrace is being captured.\nTo enable CUDA Python backtrace from Nsight Systems:\nCLI\n— Set\n--python-backtrace=cuda\n.\nGUI\n— Select the\nCollect Python backtrace for selected API calls\ncheckbox.\nExample screenshot:\nCUDA Default Functi\n\n[EXCERPT extracted-character range 192000:196000]\n work, that will also be highlighted:\nHovering over a particular OpenACC construct will bring up a tooltip with\n\ndetails about that construct:\nTo capture OpenACC information from the Nsight Systems GUI, select the\nCollect OpenACC trace\ncheckbox under\nCollect CUDA trace\nconfigurations.\n\nNote that turning on OpenACC tracing will also turn on CUDA tracing.\nPlease note that if your application crashes before all collected OpenACC trace\n\ndata has been copied out, some or all data might be lost and not present in the\n\nreport.\nOpenGL Trace\n\nOpenGL and OpenGL ES APIs can be traced to assist in the analysis of CPU and GPU interactions.\nA few usage examples are:\nVisualize how long\neglSwapBuffers\n(or similar) is taking.\nAPI trace can easily show correlations between thread state and graphics driver’s behavior, uncovering where the CPU may be waiting on the GPU.\nSpot bubbles of opportunity on the GPU, where more GPU workload could be created.\nUse\nKHR_debug\nextension to trace GL events on both the CPU and GPU.\nOpenGL trace feature in Nsight Systems consists of two different activities which will be shown in the CPU rows for those threads\nCPU trace\n: interception of API calls that an application does to APIs (such as OpenGL, OpenGL ES, EGL, GLX, WGL, etc.).\nGPU trace\n(or\nworkload trace\n): trace of GPU workload (activity) triggered by use of OpenGL or OpenGL ES. Since draw calls are executed back-to-back, the GPU workload trace ranges include many OpenGL draw calls and operations in order to optimize performance overhead, rather than tracing each individual operation.\nTo collect GPU trace, the\nglQueryCounter()\nfunction is used to measure how much time batches of GPU workload take to complete.\nRanges defined by the\nKHR_debug\ncalls are represented similarly to OpenGL API and OpenGL GPU workload trace. GPU ranges in this case represent\nincremental draw cost\n. They cannot fully account for GPUs that can execute multiple draw calls in parallel. In this case, Nsight Systems will not show overlapping GPU ranges.\nOpenGL Trace Using Command Line\n\nFor general information on using the target CLI, see\nCLI Profiling on Linux\n.
